THE APPROACHES TO AGI — the spine (v3.0)

The map of the real bets on how to build general intelligence — and the one thing this project now exists to master. Every serious idea for how you actually get to AGI/ASI is a row on this page. Each one is laid out the same way: what it bets, what it claims intelligence is, what it optimizes, and — most important — where it is stuck. This is the roadmap the system produces; the deep-dive modules fill it in.

Part of: PROJECT ASI · System Version: 3.0 (the AGI-approaches re-root) · Status: Living / revisable · Last updated: 2026-07-16 (THE BOUNDS shipped — first cross-cutting page (③ Across the approaches): data · compute · energy ceilings as a race between efficiency-receding and demand-rising; economic/supply walls, not physics walls. All eleven approach cards + the verdict + the bounds live; one cross-cutting page — alignment/RSI — remains.)

🧭 New here? Read in this order (you need no prior knowledge)

The pages in the menu are one staircase — each step uses only what the steps below already taught, and every hard word is explained where it first appears. So just go top to bottom:

  1. This map — skim it now to see the whole picture (the ~11 bets on how to reach AGI). Do not try to understand it all yet; it is a map you will come back to. It makes full sense after you have read a few approaches.
  2. ① How AI works today — two short pages that everything else builds on: 01 guessing the next word → 02 scaling laws & emergence. Start here if you are new.
  3. ② The approaches to AGI — the real bets, one page each, in order: 01 AP1 · scale → 02 AP2 · reasoning → 03 AP3 · agents → 04 AP4 · RL from interaction → 05 AP5 · world models / JEPA → 06 AP6 · brain-based → 07 AP7 · neurosymbolic / hybrid → 08 AP8 · program synthesis / ARC → 09 AP9 · open-endedness → 10 AP10 · embodiment → 11 AP11 · whole-brain emulation. (The page number = the approach's number on the map below. All eleven cards are now written — the map is complete.)
  4. ③ Across the approaches — a short thread that runs across all the bets (not a new bet): 01 The bounds — data, compute, energy. The three physical/economic ceilings every scale-based bet must respect — the arithmetic side of "is scale enough?". (A second page, alignment / self-improvement, is still to be written.)
  5. ④ The verdict — read it last, after the eleven bets and the bounds: The verdict — which bets actually get to AGI?. This is the map's capstone — it does not add a twelfth bet, it judges the eleven: the one axis they all share, the four families they fall into, a ranked read, and an honest, revisable independent viewpoint ("scale plus a stack of ingredients").

Every page opens with a "You are here" box and a one-line reminder of the pages below it, so you will never be lost. If a word is new, it is glossed (explained in plain words in brackets) right there. Take them in order and nothing will feel like a jump.

What changed on 2026-07-14 (read this first)

The project used to be learn-first: decompose the whole AI domain into ~185 modules, learn every basic and prerequisite, and treat AGI as the distant destination at the end. The learner reversed the arrow.

The new direction, in the learner's words: "My aim is to understand and get the knowledge — exactly the approaches towards AGI and ASI — so I can build a research kind of startup or be a self-research scientist. I don't want to learn unnecessary basics or topics (data engineering, system design, MLOps, ad-ranking ML — other people already build on those). I'm aiming higher than everything existing, because it's all going to be AGI. So: only the knowledge needed to achieve that. And it shouldn't feel like I'm just doing vibe coding."

So the tree is re-rooted. This page — the approaches to AGI — is the new trunk. Everything that used to be the spine (foundations, ML basics, math, compute) is demoted to a reference library you pull from only when an approach needs it. (What that means for the old files: §6.)

1. The mission, sharpened — three rules that decide everything

  1. Investigation-first, not build-first and not learn-first. The daily work is understanding the approaches to AGI/ASI well enough to judge them, extend them, and eventually do original research. We are building a map of the frontier and an independent viewpoint on it — not a curriculum, not (yet) a system.
  2. Critical-path-only. We study an idea only if not knowing it would leave us unable to judge an approach to AGI. Everything else is somebody else's job. (The exclusion list is §5. This is the project's old "rely-on-to-build" / director-altitude test, re-pointed at AGI approaches.)
  3. Grounded, not vibe. Every claim traces to a real source in the corpus (a paper, a lab's own writing, a named thinker) — verbatim where it matters. The failure we refuse: confident hand-waving that sounds like understanding. If we can't ground it, we mark it open, not settled.
  4. Fresh, not just grounded (added 2026-07-14). The corpus grounds the durable idea; but it may be stale, and the AGI frontier moves monthly. So for every module — and every concept inside it — do a live web-research pass for the current state of the art before writing: what's the latest result, has the field moved, is the "SOTA" claim still true? Corpus for grounding, the web for currency. Date every freshness check (SOTA as of YYYY-MM), because it will age. When corpus and current SOTA disagree, say so — the disagreement is often the most interesting part.

2. The root question — what even is general intelligence, and what's missing?

Every approach is secretly an answer to one question: what is intelligence, such that a machine could have it in general? The two definitions that anchor the field:

  • Legg & Hutter — intelligence is the ability to achieve goals across a wide range of environments. (Breadth of competence.) → grounds on legg in corpus.
  • Chollet — intelligence is skill-acquisition efficiency: how well you handle novel tasks you were not prepared for, per unit of prior experience. (Generalization to the unknown, not stored skill.) → grounds on measure-of-intelligence (ARC).

Hold both: a system can be broadly skilled yet brittle on anything genuinely new. That gap is the target.

What today's best systems still lack (the honest gap-list — the real research frontier):

Gap What it means Why it blocks AGI
Robust reasoning reliable multi-step inference, not fluent pattern-completion current models reason sometimes, then fail on a trivial variant
Continual / lifelong learning learn new things without forgetting old (catastrophic forgetting = new learning erases old) a mind that can't keep learning after training isn't general
Grounding & world-models connect symbols to a model of how the world actually works text-only systems "know" words, not consequences
Memory & long-horizon agency hold state and pursue goals over long stretches errors compound; reliability collapses past a few steps
Sample efficiency learn from little, like a child, not from the whole internet brute-force data may be hitting a wall
Causality & abstraction grasp why, and form reusable concepts correlation-machines interpolate; they don't reliably abstract
Autonomy set and revise its own goals current systems act only when prompted

Every approach below is a bet about which of these gaps is the real bottleneck, and how to close it.

3. The approaches — the real bets

Each is a live wager by serious people. The table is the map; the cards below carry the substance. (These become the first deep-dive modules — see §7.)

# Approach The core bet (one line) Chief proponents Where it's stuck
AP1 Scale / foundation models one self-supervised objective + massive scale → general capability; ability emerges OpenAI, Anthropic; Kaplan, Hoffmann (Chinchilla); Sutton ("Bitter Lesson") data/compute wall; is "emergence" real or a metric artifact; reasoning stays brittle
AP2 Reasoning & test-time compute make it think longer — long chains of thought + search + RL on reasoning OpenAI o-series, DeepSeek-R1 line does learned reasoning generalize past verifiable domains (math/code)?
AP3 Agents & cognitive architectures a mind is a system: model + memory + planning + tools + feedback LLM-agent field; classical SOAR/ACT-R lineage long-horizon reliability degrades; is orchestration a path or just an app layer?
AP4 RL from interaction ("reward is enough") intelligence emerges from maximizing reward in a rich world; self-play bootstraps it DeepMind (Silver, Sutton); AlphaZero line reward specification & sparsity; open, unspecified real-world goals
AP5 World models / objective-driven (JEPA) learn a predictive model of the world from sensory data, then plan against it LeCun (JEPA); Ha/Schmidhuber; active inference (Friston) can self-supervised world-prediction yield abstraction & reasoning? unproven at scale
AP6 Brain-based / neuro-grounded copy the one working example — reverse-engineer the cortex's algorithm Hawkins/Numenta (Thousand Brains); predictive coding; neuromorphic we don't yet know the brain's algorithm; how much biological detail is necessary?
AP7 Neurosymbolic / hybrid pure neural nets can't reliably reason; fuse learning with symbolic structure & search Marcus; AlphaGeometry-style hybrids how to integrate without losing learnability; learned vs. built-in structure
AP8 Program synthesis & abstraction (ARC) intelligence = efficient skill-acquisition on novelty; get it by search over programs/abstractions Chollet (ARC-AGI) search is intractable without strong priors; where do broad priors come from?
AP9 Open-endedness, evolution & continual learning AGI is produced by an open-ended process, not designed as an artifact Stanley, Clune ("AI-generating algorithms", POET) hard to define/measure open-endedness; compute-hungry; early
AP10 Embodiment & sensorimotor grounding intelligence needs a body — symbols must be grounded in physical interaction robotics-foundation-model field; grounding-problem lineage is embodiment necessary or one route? real-world data is slow & costly
AP11 Whole-brain emulation scan & simulate a brain neuron-by-neuron — no need to understand it connectomics/WBE community scanning + simulation astronomically far off; ethics

How much heat each bet has (as of 2026-07) — the "where's the action" axis

So you can pick where to dig. (This is the plausible/supported/viral read the learner asked for. It ages fast — re-check per rule #4.)

  • Mainstream — most labs, most compute, most money: AP1 (scale), AP2 (reasoning/test-time compute — now standard; every major lab ships one), AP3 (agents). This is where the GPUs actually go today.
  • Rising fast — hot, funded, still unproven at scale: AP5 (world models — "exploded into mainstream" late-2025→2026; the world-models race), AP4 (RL-from-interaction — revived by reasoning-RL).
  • Serious but contrarian — respected dissent, smaller compute: AP6 (brain-based, Hawkins), AP7 (neurosymbolic, Marcus), AP8 (program-synthesis/ARC, Chollet), AP9 (open-endedness, Clune/Stanley).
  • Rising fast (as engineering) — hot, funded, unproven as the path: AP10 (embodiment) — robotics is now one of the loudest areas in applied AI (VLAs, the humanoid wave), so the engineering is mainstream and well-funded; but embodiment as the key to general intelligence (rather than one useful output for a scaled model) stays contrarian and unproven. (Updated 2026-07-16 — this line used to file AP10 as "long-shot / fringe"; the robot-foundation-model surge moved it.)
  • Long-shot / fringe — interesting, far off: AP11 (whole-brain emulation) — the longest-horizon bet on the map; its connectomics half is real, funded science, but whole-brain emulation as a route to AGI is decades off and studied more as an idea (copy-without-understanding) than a near-term plan.

The cards (substance per approach)

Each card: Bet · Intelligence is… · Optimizes · Strongest critique · Grounding · Pulls in on demand. (Deep-dive modules expand these; this is the skeleton.)

AP1 · Scale / foundation models. Bet: predict-the-next-token at enough scale is most of the way to general intelligence; capability appears as a side-effect of compression. Intelligence is: a by-product of near-perfect prediction (predict well ⇒ compress ⇒ understand). Optimizes: loss on next-token prediction, driven by scaling laws (compute × data × parameters). Strongest critique: Schaeffer — "emergent abilities" may be an artifact of harsh metrics, not real phase-changes; Chollet/Marcus — it interpolates, it doesn't generalize to true novelty; a data wall looms. Grounding: papers/D3-scaling (Kaplan, Chinchilla), emergent-abilities, Sutton bitter-lesson, _ATLAS_ai.md. SOTA 2026-07 (web): the pretraining data wall has effectively arrived — high-quality human text is ~exhausted, so gains now come from synthetic data + verifiable-quality filtering + post-training + test-time compute, not raw pretraining scale. That shift is itself evidence the "scale-alone" bet is straining (labs keep a ~60–70% human-data anchor and add synthetic only on verifiable domains). Pulls in on demand (visible ladder): guessing the next word, scaling laws & emergence. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP1 · Scale & foundation models (the pilot cluster; proof-of-standard for the new simple, incremental approach-module method).

AP2 · Reasoning & test-time compute. Bet: the missing piece is deliberation — spend compute at inference (long chain-of-thought, search, self-verify) and train it with RL on reasoning traces. Intelligence is: search + verification, not one fast forward pass. Optimizes: correctness on verifiable problems, then hopefully transfer. Strongest critique: works where answers are checkable (math, code); unclear it becomes general judgment; may be elaborate retrieval — and 2025 work (Yue et al.) argues RL only sharpens the base model rather than creating new reasoning (base beats RL at large pass@k). Grounding: papers/D3-scaling (chain-of-thought 2201.11903, 2501.12948_deepseek-r1), D13 survey-reasoning-foundation-models, D5-rl (AlphaZero self-play, PPO — the durable RL ancestor). SOTA 2026-07 (web): now the frontier paradigm — every major lab ships a reasoning model; OpenAI o3 at high test-time compute hit 87.5% on ARC-AGI-1 (semi-private; ~$3.5k/task, 172× the compute of its 75.7% low-compute run) in Dec 2024; DeepSeek-R1 (2025) showed strong reasoning emerges from pure RL (GRPO, verifiable rewards) with no labeled reasoning traces; inference compute has overtaken training (>55% of AI infra spend, early 2026). But the honest novelty gap: on the harder ARC-AGI-2 (built to resist memory), the best pure reasoning model reached only ~37.6% (Opus 4.5, Dec 2025, ARC Prize) — and ARC Prize found gains are "tied to model knowledge" / training-data overlap, not fluid reasoning. This card has moved from "emerging" to co-leading with AP1. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP2 · Reasoning & test-time compute. Pulls in: RL (deep home = future AP4), verifiers/reward models, search, chain-of-thought → AP2.

AP3 · Agents & cognitive architectures. Bet: a single model isn't a mind — wrap it in memory, planning, tools, and loops (maybe many models). Intelligence is: an architecture, a system of parts. Optimizes: task completion over long horizons. Strongest critique: errors compound → long-horizon reliability collapses; is this AGI research or application engineering? Grounding: the published agent systems (verbatim — ReAct 2210.03629, Reflexion 2303.11366, Generative Agents 2304.03442, Voyager 2305.16291, all in papers/D9-agents-rag), Karpathy 2025 (the "decade of agents," "march of nines"), classical cognitive-architecture history (SOAR/ACT-R). SOTA 2026-07 (web): the mainstream bet — every capable AI deployment is now an agent system, and the capability is measurably climbing: METR's task time horizon (the length of job an agent finishes on its own at 50% reliability) has doubled ~every 7 months since 2019 (Claude 3.7 Sonnet ~1 hr, Mar 2025), reportedly speeding to ~4 months post-2023 with frontier horizons of several hours by early 2026 (secondary trackers — treat hour-counts as soft). 2026 measuring-sticks: SWE-bench Verified, GAIA, OSWorld/computer-use. But the live crux: the 2024-era orchestrator-critic scaffolding is dissolving into the models — a single agentic model now plans, remembers, and self-checks inside its own chain of thought — which is Sutton's Bitter Lesson pointed at AP3, and raises the sharpest doubt: is "agent-building" a road to AGI or a temporary app layer? ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP3 · Agents & cognitive architectures — the deep home for the agent idea; the reasoning brain inside it is AP2. Pulls in: the reasoning core → AP2; the Bitter-Lesson threat → AP1; the reward-driven agent → AP4; the reasoning ceiling → AP8.

AP4 · RL from interaction ("reward is enough"). Bet: put an agent in a rich world with a reward and general abilities emerge; self-play can bootstrap superhuman skill. Intelligence is: whatever maximizes long-run reward. Optimizes: cumulative reward. Strongest critique: who writes the reward? sparse/mis-specified reward breaks it (reward hacking); sample-inefficient; open real-world goals resist a single scalar. Grounding: papers/D5-rl (DQN, AlphaZero, OpenAI Five, PPO), Sutton & Barto textbook (reward hypothesis), Silver et al. "Reward is Enough" 2021 (via live web — not in corpus). SOTA 2026-07 (web): RL is the other live frontier bet — Sutton & Barto won the 2024 Turing Award (announced Mar 2025) right as reward-learning came roaring back inside reasoning models (AP2); Silver & Sutton's 2025 "Welcome to the Era of Experience" reframes AP4 as the answer to AP1's data wall (learn from your own experience + grounded rewards, not human text). Still: the pure "reward alone grows a whole mind from scratch in the open world" claim is unproven — every clean win is a fast, cheap-reward simulator. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP4 · RL from interaction — the deep home for reinforcement learning (the full frame AP2 only tasted). Pulls in: RL frame (self-contained here), self-play, exploration; forward to AP5 (world models) & alignment.

AP5 · World models / objective-driven (JEPA). Bet: autoregressive text is a dead end for real understanding; learn a predictive world-model from mostly-sensory data (video) and plan against an objective. Intelligence is: a model of the world good enough to plan with. Optimizes: prediction of future world-states in a learned abstract space (JEPA = predict the summary, not the pixels). Strongest critique: still largely unproven at scale; can it produce abstraction & language-level reasoning? — and maybe text already carries the world-model (the AP1 fight). Grounding: Lex × LeCun 2024 (verbatim — four missing pieces, sensory-bandwidth, autoregressive drift, world-model def, JEPA "windy leaves," hierarchical planning unsolved), Ha & Schmidhuber World Models 2018 (papers/D11-robotics/1803.10122 — the "dream" root), lecun corpus. SOTA 2026-07 (web): the "world-models race" — Meta V-JEPA 2 (Jun 2025, zero-shot robot planning from web video); DreamerV3 in Nature (2025, first to mine Minecraft diamonds from scratch — world-model + RL, the AP4 bridge); LeCun left Meta (Nov 2025) to found AMI Labs, which raised ~$1.03B (Mar 2026); DeepMind Genie 3 (Aug 2025) & Fei-Fei Li's World Labs / Marble (late-2025 → $1B Feb 2026). No longer fringe. (Caution: "world model" now names two opposite bets — generative pixel-drawers (Genie/Marble) vs LeCun's non-generative JEPA, which argues the generative kind is the wrong path; and some 2026 "reasoning found inside" press claims trace to weak sources — keep those open.) ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP5 · World models / JEPA — the deep home for the world-model idea AP4 pointed at. Pulls in: the RL frame & sample-inefficiency → AP4; the scaling-suffices fight → AP1; the text machine → next-word.

AP6 · Brain-based / neuro-grounded. Bet: the brain is the only existence proof — reverse-engineer its algorithm (cortical columns, reference frames, constant prediction). Intelligence is: what the neocortex does. Optimizes: biological faithfulness / predictive modeling of sensory input. Strongest critique: we don't yet understand the algorithm; unclear how much biological detail is essential vs. distraction; and the brain-ignoring approaches (AP1) are years ahead. Grounding: Hawkins A Thousand Brains (2021, verbatim — Mountcastle's one-algorithm idea, models in reference frames, the "columns vote" Thousand Brains Theory, the four attributes of an intelligent machine), hawkins (101 hits). SOTA 2026-07 (web): the bet is now real code & silicon — Numenta's Thousand Brains Project (open-source sensorimotor framework, Nov 2024; independent nonprofit Jan 2025) + neuromorphic chips (Intel Loihi 2, IBM NorthPole; big reported energy savings on narrow tasks, but no production general-purpose product shipped). Still: no brain-based system is remotely as capable as an LLM. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP6 · Brain-based — pairs with AP5 (the brain as a biological world model) and answers AP8's "where do broad priors come from?" Pulls in: the text machine → next-word; the world-model cousin → AP5; the flexibility target → AP8.

AP7 · Neurosymbolic / hybrid. Bet: connectionism alone can't reliably reason or generalize compositionally; keep the neural learner and bolt on a symbolic reasoner (rules, logic, knowledge, exact checking) — join the two. Intelligence is: learning + explicit reasoning, together. Optimizes: robustness — behaving systematically & reliably, and transferring across contexts (Marcus's "robust AI"). Strongest critique: the integration is unsolved (no gradient flows through discrete logic); is the structure learned or hand-built (and does hand-built scale — the Bitter Lesson)?; and "neurosymbolic" is a big tent, hard to falsify. Grounding: the Marcus–Lamb Machine Learning Street Talk conversation (marcus, verbatim), Marcus's "The Next Decade in AI" (2020), Fodor & Pylyshyn (1988) systematicity; the working example = AlphaGeometry. SOTA 2026-07 (web): the bet is reviving — AlphaGeometry (neural guesses the construction, symbolic engine proves it) hit 25/30 Olympiad geometry ≈ gold-medalist (Jan 2024); AlphaGeometry 2 + AlphaProof reached IMO silver (4/6, 2024; Nature 2025); and the frontier is quietly de-facto neurosymbolic — reliable models now call calculators, code interpreters, theorem provers, databases (neural proposes, symbolic tool checks). The live crux: is that a real distinct road, or the Bitter Lesson temporarily borrowing a crutch scale will absorb? — the same "learning vs built-in structure" fight as AP3/AP8. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP7 · Neurosymbolic / hybrid AI — the deep home for neurosymbolic; AP8 is its sharpest special case. Pulls in: the Bitter Lesson → AP1; fast/slow + checkable domains → AP2; program synthesis → AP8.

AP8 · Program synthesis & abstraction (ARC). Bet: measure intelligence as efficient generalization to novel tasks (ARC-AGI), and pursue it by searching over programs/abstractions, not by scaling. Intelligence is: skill-acquisition efficiency on the unknown. Optimizes: solving unseen tasks from few examples. Strongest critique: program search explodes without strong priors — so where do broad priors come from (hybrid with LLMs)? — and o3 (Dec 2024) did beat ARC-AGI-1 with expensive test-time search, on a scaled model. Grounding: the Dwarkesh × Chollet 2024 conversation (verbatim — fetching-vs-synthesis, skill≠intelligence, ARC's design, the deep-learning + program-search merger, System 1/2, test-time fine-tuning), Chollet measure-of-intelligence. SOTA 2026-07 (web): ARC-AGI-1 is beaten — o3 hit 75.7% / 87.5% (low/high compute, ~$3.5k/task, Dec 2024), ~98% frontier by early 2026 — but the efficiency the bet cares about is terrible. So the team keeps raising the bar: ARC-AGI-2 (2025) best verified frontier ~37.6% (Opus 4.5) / efficiency-track ~24% vs humans ~60%; ARC-AGI-3 (2026) frontier <1% vs humans 100%. The winning methods are exactly AP8's — program search + test-time training. The versions-keep-falling pattern is itself the open question (is ARC a fixed target, or a moving one?). ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP8 · Program synthesis / ARC — the deep home for "skill is not intelligence," which AP1 only touched. Pulls in: the interpolative-memory machine → next-word; the scaling line it argues against → AP1; test-time compute → AP2.

AP9 · Open-endedness, evolution & continual learning. Bet: general intelligence came from an open-ended process (evolution → culture); engineer systems that endlessly generate their own novel challenges and never stop learning. Intelligence is: the output of a process, not a fixed artifact. Optimizes: sustained novelty / diversity, not a fixed objective. Strongest critique: "open-endedness" is hard to define or measure; extremely compute-hungry & prone to stall; and its recent wins run on foundation models (road, or a loop bolted onto scale?). Grounding: Kenneth Stanley & Joel Lehman Why Greatness Cannot Be Planned (2015) via the Machine Learning Street Talk Stanley conversation (verbatim — "objectives themselves block discovery," "unknown stepping stones are portals…," deception, Picbreeder) + the Tim Rocktäschel conversation (verbatim — open-endedness = artifacts "both novel and learnable"). SOTA 2026-07 (web): the bet went mainstream in 2024–26 — Google DeepMind's position paper "Open-Endedness is Essential for Artificial Superhuman Intelligence" (2406.04268, ICML 2024) put it on a top lab's roadmap; novelty search / quality-diversity (MAP-Elites) / POET (1901.01753) are the classic tools; AI-generating algorithms (Clune) the boldest framing; and foundation models finally gave "interesting" a usable measure (OMNI), producing real wins — AlphaEvolve (2506.13131, May 2025) found a 4×4 complex matrix-mult using 48 multiplications, beating Strassen 1969; the Darwin Gödel Machine (2505.22954, May 2025) self-improved its coding agents 20%→50% on SWE-bench. The live crux: those wins are open-ended loops wrapped around foundation models — so is this a distinct road, or scale's outer loop? (the same fight as AP3/AP7/AP10). ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP9 · Open-endedness & evolution — the deep home for open-endedness, the objective paradox, novelty search, quality-diversity, POET, and AI-GAs. Pulls in: the reward/self-play/reward-hacking frame → AP4 (AP9 answers its "who writes the reward?"); the handling-novelty cousin → AP8; the Bitter Lesson + data wall + foundation models → AP1.

AP10 · Embodiment & sensorimotor grounding. Bet: a mind needs a body — meaning is grounded in acting on the physical world. Intelligence is: grounded sensorimotor competence. Optimizes: real-world perception-action performance. Strongest critique: necessity vs. sufficiency (is embodiment required, or one path?); physical data is slow and expensive. Grounding: the symbol grounding problem lineage (SEP Chinese Room / the Robot Reply, Harnad; Moravec's paradox; Brooks "the world is its own best model" / "Intelligence Without Representation" 1991 via christian-alignment) + the VLA papers (papers/D11-robotics — RT-2 2307.15818 verbatim, PaLM-E 2303.03378, OpenVLA 2406.09246, π0 2410.24164). SOTA 2026-07 (web): the hottest area in applied AI — the robot foundation model / vision-language-action (VLA) recipe (web-trained model + robot actions as tokens) went from RT-2 (2023) to π0 / π0.5 / π0.7 (Physical Intelligence — one generalist model driving several robot bodies) and Gemini Robotics 1.5 (DeepMind — a VLA that "thinks before acting," cross-embodiment transfer), while a humanoid wave (Figure/Helix, Tesla Optimus, 1X NEO, Apptronik, on Nvidia GR00T) moved into early paid pilots. But the live crux: the winning robots are ordinary large models with actions bolted on — so is embodiment a distinct road or scale's last mile? — the same Bitter-Lesson fight as AP3/AP7/AP8; and 2026 work ("Intelligence Requires Grounding But Not Embodiment") argues symbols need grounding but maybe not a body (a bodiless model rides on our already-grounded text — "epistemic parasitism"). The other wall is data: no internet of robot actions, plus the sim-to-real and human-to-robot gaps. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP10 · Embodiment & sensorimotor grounding — the deep home for embodiment & the symbol grounding problem; the embodied home for the agent AP3 pointed at, and AP5's action-side partner. Pulls in: the world-model / sensory-bandwidth partner → AP5; learning-through-movement → AP6; trial-and-error + sample-inefficiency → AP4; the embodied agent → AP3; the Bitter Lesson & foundation models → AP1.

AP11 · Whole-brain emulation. Bet: skip understanding — scan a real brain in full detail and simulate it directly; a mind runs because the copy is faithful, whether or not anyone understands why. The opposite twin of AP6: copy everything (understand nothing) vs extract the algorithm (understand the method). Intelligence is: a particular physical pattern of activity — copied, not theorised. Optimizes: fidelity of the emulation. Strongest critique: a connectome (wiring diagram) is not a running brain (the 302-neuron worm is mapped yet still unsimulated); scanning + simulating a whole human brain is far beyond current tech; and it is the slowest path, yields a mind we still can't explain, and carries deep identity/consciousness/suffering ethics. Grounding: Sandberg & Bostrom, "Whole Brain Emulation: A Roadmap" (2008, FHI) — the definition (verbatim), the "no understanding of intelligence required" thesis, scan→translate→simulate, scale-separation; connectomics + neuroscience corpus + compute-limits. SOTA 2026-07 (web): the connectome ladder is real and accelerating — C. elegans (302 neurons, mapped 1986; BAAIWorm 2026 still can't learn), the FlyWire adult fruit-fly connectome (~140k neurons, >50M synapses, Nature Oct 2024), MICrONS mouse cubic-mm (84k neurons, >500M synapses + function, Apr 2025), a Harvard–Google human cubic-mm (16k neurons, 150M synapses); expansion microscopy (LICONN ~20 nm, 2025) + AI tracing; NIH BRAIN CONNECTS ($150M, 2023). But: a whole human brain (86B neurons, ~100T synapses) is a million-plus times the biggest speck mapped; scanning is destructive; compute estimates span 10^18–10^30+ FLOPS (scale-separation unknown); and the flagship Human Brain Project (~€1B, 2013–2023) and Blue Brain Project (ended Dec 2024) under-delivered. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → AP11 · Whole-brain emulation — the deep home for whole-brain emulation, mind uploading, and the connectome; AP6's opposite twin. Pulls in: the brain-as-existence-proof + "right but too slow" → AP6; substrate-independence / functionalism → AP10; the faster rival that likely arrives first → AP1.

4. Cross-cutting — on the path to ASI (not plumbing; genuinely load-bearing)

Three threads sit across the approaches. They stay on the critical path because they decide whether any approach reaches AGI, and whether AGI tips into ASI.

  • The bounds — scaling laws, the data wall, compute & energy. Every approach's feasibility is gated by these. Not "infrastructure practice" — the physical limits that decide which bets can even run. → papers/D3-scaling, hardware-compute, energy corpus. ▶ DEEP-DIVE WRITTEN → The bounds — data, compute, energy (reading group ③; the first cross-cutting page — the three ceilings as a race, economic/supply walls not physics walls).
  • Alignment, control & recursive self-improvement (RSI). "Toward ASI" is incoherent without control; and RSI / the intelligence explosion is itself a path claim (a system that improves itself). → papers/D4-alignment, D14-surveys-alignment-safety, governance-safety, Anthropic frontier-rnd.
  • The scaling-suffices debate. The field's central live crux: does AP1 alone get there, or is a second ingredient (AP2/AP5/AP7…) required? Everything else is a position in this argument.

5. What we deliberately do NOT study (the exclusion list)

Per rule #2 (critical-path-only) and the learner's explicit ask. These are real, valuable, and someone else's job:

  • Production ML plumbing — data engineering, pipelines, MLOps, model serving/deployment, monitoring, infra-ops.
  • System-design / distributed-systems craft — except where it is a hard bound on AGI (compute, energy, memory-bandwidth walls).
  • Applied / vertical ML on top of existing models — recommender systems, ad ranking, most "AI for X" products.
  • Framework & tooling churn — which library, which API, which vendor SDK.
  • Coding & implementation grind — the AI does the typing; the learner directs and decides.

The one-line test for anything new: "Would not knowing this leave me unable to judge or extend an approach to AGI?" If no → skip it, or drop a one-line pointer and move on. If yes → it earns a place, pulled in behind the approach that needs it.

6. What happens to the old project (the re-root, concretely)

Nothing valuable is thrown away — its role changes.

Asset Old role New role
00_MAP.md, CURRICULUM.md the driving spine (learn-first) legacy / reference index. Superseded by this page as the spine.
The written modules (0100–1310) the curriculum to complete on-demand reference library. Read a module only when an approach card links to it. No obligation to "finish" them.
../RESOURCES/corpus/ + _ATLAS_ai.md grounding for all ~185 modules grounding for the approaches — unchanged and central; the corpus already maps onto §3 (see the anchor check).
CONCEPT_REGISTRY.md the no-repeat ledger unchanged — still the one place each concept is owned.
The content standard (../INSTRUCTIONS/HARD_RULES.md) how every module is written unchanged — grounded, DRY, simple-words-deep-ideas, glossed. The deep-dive modules obey it.

The broad "cover every field" branches (70-sciences/, 80-engineering/, most of 40-compute beyond the bounds) are parked — they were the "unnecessary basics" the learner is pruning. Pull one back only if an approach genuinely needs it.

(The instruction docs that still say "learn-first / ~185 modules" — MASTER_INSTRUCTION, PRODUCTION_FLOW, LEARNER_STRATEGY — get a v3.0 alignment pass next session. This page is the source of truth until they catch up.)

7. The production order (what to write next → feeds _QUEUE.md)

Deep-dive one approach at a time, each a grounded module in 20-the-approaches/, written to the HARD_RULES standard, linking down to reference rungs on demand. Ordered by leverage × how live the bet is × corpus-readiness:

  1. ~~AP1 · Scale / foundation models~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-14) — the bet everything else argues with; corpus is richest here (D3, Sutton, Chinchilla, emergence). Written as the pilot cluster (proof-of-standard): AP1 · Scale + its mechanism home scaling laws & emergence. Every later card can now say "…versus scaling." H·H·✅
  2. ~~AP2 · Reasoning & test-time compute~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-14) — the co-leading frontier bet of right now; grounded in the Chain-of-Thought paper, DeepSeek-R1, and AlphaZero, with a full live-SOTA pass. Written as AP2 · Reasoning; builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1. H·H·✅

2b. ~~AP4 · RL from interaction~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-14) — the other live frontier bet, and the deep home for the reward-learning idea AP2 only touched (Leg 3). Written as AP4 · RL from interaction; the full RL frame + the "reward is enough" bet + four cracks. Grounded in Sutton & Barto, DQN, OpenAI Five, AlphaZero (via AP2), "Reward is Enough" (2021), with a live-SOTA pass (Turing Award, Era of Experience). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP2. It repaid AP2's forward-reference. H·H·✅

  1. ~~AP5 · World models / JEPA~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-14) — the strongest architectural dissent from AP1, and the deep home for the world-model idea AP4 pointed at (plan inside a learned model instead of paying for real tries). Written as AP5 · World models / JEPA; grounded verbatim in Lex × LeCun 2024 + Ha & Schmidhuber 2018, with a full live-SOTA pass (V-JEPA 2, DreamerV3 in Nature, AMI Labs' $1.03B, Genie 3, World Labs). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP4; repaid AP4's forward-reference. H·H·✅
  2. ~~AP8 · Program synthesis / ARC~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-15) — the deepest "scale won't do it" bet, and the deep home for skill ≠ intelligence (which AP1 only touched). Written as AP8 · Program synthesis / ARC; grounded verbatim in the Dwarkesh × Chollet 2024 conversation + "On the Measure of Intelligence," with a full live-SOTA pass (o3 beat ARC-AGI-1; ARC-AGI-2/-3 restored the gap; program-search + test-time-training are the winning methods). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP2. H·H·✅

4b. ~~AP6 · Brain-based~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-15) — the "copy the one thing that works" bet. Written as AP6 · Brain-based; grounded verbatim in Hawkins' A Thousand Brains (Mountcastle's one-algorithm idea, models in reference frames, the Thousand Brains "columns vote" theory, the four attributes) + a live-SOTA pass (Thousand Brains Project open-sourced; neuromorphic chips). Pairs with AP5 (biological world model) and answers AP8's Stuck #1 (where priors come from). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP5 + AP8. H·H·✅ 4c. ~~AP3 · Agents & cognitive architectures~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-15) — the mainstream bet (completes the AP1·AP2·AP3 mainstream trio) and the deep home for the agent idea. Written as AP3 · Agents & cognitive architectures; the "a mind is a system of parts" bet (act-loop/tools · memory+reflection · planning · skill-library), grounded verbatim in the published agent systems (ReAct, Reflexion, Generative Agents, Voyager) + Karpathy 2025, with a full live-SOTA pass (METR task-horizon doubling; the "scaffolding gets absorbed by the model" Bitter-Lesson debate surfaced as the central open question). Four cracks: compounding errors / march of nines · scaffolding-vs-model (road or app layer?) · can't out-think its core model · multi-agent unproven. Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP2; sits before AP4 in reading order (forward-references it). H·H·✅ 4d. ~~AP7 · Neurosymbolic / hybrid AI~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-16) — the general "join a neural learner to a symbolic reasoner" family, and the deep home for neurosymbolic AI (AP8 is its ARC-focused special case). Written as AP7 · Neurosymbolic / hybrid AI; grounded verbatim in the Marcus–Lamb Machine Learning Street Talk conversation + Marcus's "The Next Decade in AI" (2020) + Fodor & Pylyshyn (1988) systematicity, with a full live-SOTA pass (AlphaGeometry 25/30 ≈ gold; AlphaGeometry 2 + AlphaProof = IMO silver, Nature 2025; the de-facto-neurosymbolic LLM+tools turn). Four cracks: the integration problem (no gradient through discrete logic) · the Bitter Lesson / "just a crutch scale absorbs" · wins trapped in tidy checkable domains · hard to falsify. Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP2; reads between AP6 and AP8, forward-pointing to AP8 as its sharpest special case. M·H·✅ 4e. ~~AP10 · Embodiment & sensorimotor grounding~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-16) — the "a mind needs a body" bet, and the deep home for the symbol grounding problem; the embodied home for the agent AP3 pointed at, and AP5's action-side partner. Written as AP10 · Embodiment; grounded in the SEP Chinese Room / Robot Reply + Harnad + Moravec's paradox + Brooks "the world is its own best model" (via christian-alignment) + the RT-2 VLA paper (verbatim), with a full live-SOTA pass (π0/Gemini Robotics/OpenVLA, the humanoid wave, the sim-to-real data bottleneck, the grounding-vs-embodiment debate). Four cracks: necessity vs sufficiency (a bodiless model got far on our grounded text) · the data problem (no internet of actions; sim-to-real / human-to-robot gaps) · the Bitter Lesson (winning robots are ordinary large models with actions bolted on — road or last mile?) · hard to falsify ("body" is slippery). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP5 (closest partner) + AP4 + AP6 + AP3. M·H·✅ 4f. ~~AP9 · Open-endedness & evolution~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-16) — the "grow a mind, don't design it" bet, and the deep home for the objective paradox / novelty search / open-endedness. Written as AP9 · Open-endedness & evolution; grounded verbatim in Stanley & Lehman Why Greatness Cannot Be Planned (via the MLST Stanley conversation) + the Rocktäschel conversation (open-endedness = novel + learnable), with a full live-SOTA pass (DeepMind's "Open-Endedness is Essential for ASI" position paper; novelty search / quality-diversity / POET / AI-GAs; OMNI; AlphaEvolve beat Strassen 1969; Darwin Gödel Machine 20%→50%). Four cracks: can't define/measure open-endedness or interestingness · road or a loop bolted onto scale? (Bitter Lesson) · compute-hungry + tends to stall · least-controllable / safety (the distinctive one). Builds on the base rungs (①) + AP1 + AP4 (answers its "who writes the reward?") + AP8 (novelty). Reads before AP10, filling the last gap. M·H·✅

  1. ~~AP11 (whole-brain emulation)~~ ✅ DONE (2026-07-16) — ⭐ THE MAP IS COMPLETE; all 11 approach cards are now live. The literal/extreme cousin (and opposite twin) of AP6 — copy the brain in full detail instead of extracting its algorithm; understand nothing. Written as AP11 · Whole-brain emulation; grounded in Sandberg & Bostrom's "Whole Brain Emulation: A Roadmap" (2008) (definition verbatim; the no-understanding thesis, scan→translate→simulate, scale-separation) with a full live-SOTA pass (the connectome ladder — C. elegans/BAAIWorm, FlyWire fly 2024, MICrONS mouse + Harvard–Google human specks 2025; expansion microscopy; the compute/scale wall; Blue Brain / Human Brain Project under-delivery; the identity/consciousness ethics).   3. Synthesis: economic/supply walls, not physics walls → a race between the ceiling receding (algorithmic efficiency) and demand rising (Jevons paradox). Status: Living feed (newest on top) · Last updated: 2026-07-16 (v3.0 · THE BOUNDS shipped — first cross-cutting page (③): data · compute · energy ceilings as a race, economic/supply walls not physics walls)

Format: DATE · [new|updated] MODULE (where / rev) — what & why — 👉 read this - [new] = a brand-new module → read the whole (usually short) file. - [updated] = an existing module changed → read only its ## Revision notes line + the one section it points to.   • 2026-07-16 · [new] The bounds — data, compute, energy (rev 1) — the first cross-cutting page — a fact that runs across all eleven bets, not a new bet. New reading group ③ Across the approaches (the verdict moves to ④). The mainstream bet is "make it bigger" (AP1) — but "bigger" burns three limited, physical things, each near a ceiling. Wall 1 · data (the fuel): good human text is a one-time supply — Epoch AI puts the useful stock at ~300 trillion tokens, projected used up around 2028 (the estimate already slipped from 2024, pushed back by cleverer data use). This is the quantity behind the data-wall idea the scaling page owns; the three data-wall answers (AP4/AP9/AP10) are really scale's new fuel lines past it. Wall 2 · compute (the engine): training compute has doubled ~every 6 months since 2010 (Sevilla 2022) — ~4× faster than chips improve — so scale is bought with money & machines, and a single frontier run's price is climbing toward $1 billion by ~2027 (Dwarkesh's counter: still <1% of world GDP of headroom). Wall 3 · energy (the power): AI data-centres used ~1.5% of world electricity in 2024, doubling toward ~3% by 2030 (IEA) — so companies are buying nuclear plants (Microsoft–Three Mile Island, Meta, Amazon, Google). The honest ending: none of the three is a physics wall — the true floor (the Landauer limit) is a billion-fold below us, and the brain runs general intelligence on ~20 watts — they are economic and supply walls that recede as we get cleverer (algorithmic efficiency halves the compute for a fixed skill every ~8 months) even as demand pushes into them faster (the Jevons paradox). So "is scale enough?" becomes a race: does the money-and-electricity ceiling arrive before scale reaches AGI, or does efficiency keep pushing it back? A bet can be right about intelligence and still lose to arithmetic. Grounded verbatim (Sevilla, MacKay's "numbers, not adjectives," Amodei, Dwarkesh) + a full live-SOTA pass (2026-07). 👉 read it — the physical floor under the whole map, and the deep version of the verdict's data-wall gauge.

  • New reading group ③ Across the approaches added (30-across-the-approaches/, reads after ② The approaches and before the verdict, which becomes ④). Registry: the three bounds, the data stock, the compute trend / training-compute doubling, the cost wall, the energy wall, the Landauer limit, algorithmic efficiency, Jevons paradox, economic/supply walls not physics walls now ✅ with home c-bounds. Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI: §4 bounds thread marked written + linked, §7 item 7a marked done (7b = the alignment/RSI page is the one remaining cross-cutting write), "New here" order gains ③ (verdict → ④), grounding ledger + status line updated. Verdict [footer + "watch the gauges"] relinked to the now-written bounds page.

  • 2026-07-16 · [new] The verdict — which bets actually get to AGI? (rev 1) — ⭐ the map's capstone — the independent viewpoint the whole project was built to reach. With all eleven bets written, this page does the thing the map was for: it judges them. It is not a twelfth bet — it adds no new facts, only a synthesis drawn across the eleven cards. Three moves. (1) The map has one axis. Almost every card ended on the same doubt — "is this a separate road to AGI, or a temporary layer a big enough model will absorb?" (AP3, AP5, AP7, AP8, AP9, AP10 all end there; AP6/AP11 on its cousin, "right but too slow"). So the eleven only look different: underneath is one question, the scaling-suffices debate — is scale the source of intelligence, or only the fuel? (2) The eleven are four families that combine, not eleven horses that race. The mainstream stack (AP1+AP2+AP3+AP4) is not four bets but four layers of one machine — a big pretrained model, that thinks in steps, wrapped in memory+tools, trained on reward — i.e. what every frontier lab ships. The second-ingredient dissents (AP5/AP7/AP8/AP9/AP10) each name a real gap (grounding · reliable reasoning · true novelty · endless learning · physical meaning) — but the decisive 2026 fact is that every checkable win they have runs on top of a big model, not instead of one, so they are being absorbed as ingredients, not winning as separate paradigms. The data-wall answers (AP4/AP9/AP10) are really one thing — the fuel supply once human text runs out. The brain bets (AP6/AP11) are long-horizon insurance. (3) A ranked read + the viewpoint. Bets are ranked by "how likely to be part of the path" (engine / ingredient / fuel), not "win alone": the mainstream stack top (near-certain), the dissents in the middle (real gaps, unproven as separate roads), the brain bets last. The viewpoint: scale plus a stack of ingredients — scale keeps absorbing its challengers (the Bitter Lesson repeating), but two named gaps stay open — true novelty (AP8/AP7) and the data wall (AP1, answered by AP4/AP9/AP10) — so "scale alone" is unproven; hold your confidence in the middle and watch those two gauges. Closes with the two opposite ways the viewpoint breaks (skeptics have lost to scale for a decade → I may over-rate the dissents; but the walls are measured and arriving → I may over-rate scale) and why the honest answer is the uncertain middle plus the exact evidence that would move it. Framed throughout as a revisable research position, not a fact. 👉 read it last — it turns the map from a reference into a thesis.

  • New reading group ③ The verdict added to the reader (40-the-verdict/, reads after ② The approaches). Registry: the one axis / "is scale the source or the fuel?", the four families of bets, scale-plus-ingredients (the viewpoint), ranking the bets by "part of the path" now ✅ with home c-verdict at The verdict. Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI: §7 item 6 (judge the map) marked done, "New here" order gains ③ (read last), status line updated; the remaining writes are the two cross-cutting modules (scaling-bounds + alignment/RSI) the verdict already leans on — the learner asked for all of them, one by one, across sessions. The map is complete and now judged — eleven bets + the verdict.

  • 2026-07-16 · [new] AP11 · Whole-brain emulation (rev 1) — ⭐ the last card — with it, all eleven bets on the map are written. The "copy it without understanding it" bet. Every other approach tries to work out what intelligence is and build it. AP11 gives up on understanding entirely: point at the one machine already generally intelligent — a human brain — scan it in full detail (every cell, every connection) and copy that exact structure into a computer. Run the copy and a mind wakes up — even though no one understands how the brain makes a mind. You don't build intelligence; you photocopy it. It is the opposite twin of AP6: AP6 is the watchmaker (understand the brain's algorithm, rebuild it, drop the biology); AP11 is the photocopier (copy everything, understand nothing) — the purest test on the map of whether understanding is even needed to build a mind. Grounded in Sandberg & Bostrom's "Whole Brain Emulation: A Roadmap" (2008, FHI Oxford — the definition verbatim: "take a particular brain, scan its structure in detail, and construct a software model of it that is so faithful … it will behave in essentially the same way as the original brain"; plus its central thesis that WBE, unlike other routes, needs no theory of intelligence, and its one big scale-separation assumption — that there's a level of detail that is "enough"). The plan is three steps: scan → translate → simulate. Live-SOTA'd (July 2026): the connectome ladder is real and accelerating — the worm C. elegans (302 neurons, mapped 1986), the FlyWire fruit-fly brain (~140k neurons, >50M synapses, Nature Oct 2024), specks of mouse (MICrONS — 84k neurons, >500M synapses + function, Apr 2025) and human brain (Harvard–Google cubic-mm, 16k neurons, 150M synapses), with expansion microscopy and AI tracing speeding it up. Then an honest judge-it with four steep cracks — a wiring diagram is not a running brain (the 302-neuron worm is fully mapped yet still unsimulated — a connectome is "wires with the components rubbed out"; strength, sign, chemistry, live state all missing) · the scale is beyond us (a whole human brain — 86B neurons, ~100T synapses — is a million-plus times the biggest speck mapped; the flagship Human Brain Project ~€1B and Blue Brain Project, ended Dec 2024, both under-delivered) · even success is slow and teaches nothing (likely arrives last, and hands you a mind you understand no better than before) · the ethics (is the copy you? is it conscious — could a failed run be a suffering mind? — the distinctive crack no other bet faces). 👉 read it — the strangest and longest-horizon bet, and the one that completes the map.

  • Registry updated: whole-brain emulation (the bet) / mind uploading, connectome / connectomics, scan → translate → simulate, connectome ≠ a running brain, scale separation, substrate independence, the copy problem / identity & consciousness are now ✅ with home at AP11 (11_ap11-whole-brain-emulation) — the deep home for whole-brain emulation, mind uploading, and the connectome. All eleven approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP3 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP7 · AP8 · AP9 · AP10 · AP11).

  • 2026-07-16 · [new] AP9 · Open-endedness & evolution (rev 1) — the "grow a mind, don't design it" bet. Every approach so far tries to design a mind — pick a goal, build a system, push it toward the goal. AP9 (Kenneth Stanley, Jeff Clune, Tim Rocktäschel) says aiming is the mistake: the one process that certainly built a general mind — evolution — had no goal; it just kept producing new, interesting living things forever, and intelligence fell out. So don't build the mind; build a process that never stops inventing (an open-ended process) and let a mind grow out of it. Its root is the objective paradox (from Stanley & Lehman's Why Greatness Cannot Be Planned): aiming at a far, strange goal can block you, because the stepping stones to a great thing don't look like the thing (vacuum tubes → computers), so a goal-compass steers you right past them — "objectives themselves block discovery." The fix is novelty search (reward new/different, not good-at-the-goal — it beats goal-chasing on deceptive mazes) and Picbreeder (aimless, interestingness-following image-breeding reaches a skull nobody aimed at). Then the modern research program: open-endedness = producing artifacts that are forever "both novel and learnable" (Rocktäschel/DeepMind — not a noisy TV, not a finished coin set); the tools quality-diversity / MAP-Elites, POET (generate the problems too — an automatic curriculum, answering AP4's "who writes the reward?"), and AI-generating algorithms (design the process that generates intelligence — Clune). Three legs — the only thing that ever built a mind is an open-ended process (evolution, then culture) · the objective paradox is real and demonstrated (novelty search beats goal-search on deceptive problems) · it is producing real current wins, made far more powerful by foundation models (DeepMind's "Open-Endedness is Essential for Artificial Superhuman Intelligence" 2024; OMNI gives "interesting" a usable measure; AlphaEvolve beat Strassen's 1969 matrix-mult record; the Darwin Gödel Machine self-improved its own code 20%→50%, 2025). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — you can't define or measure the thing it optimises (novelty/interestingness/open-endedness all depend on who's watching) · road or a loop bolted onto scale? (its wins run on foundation models — the Bitter-Lesson question) · enormously expensive and prone to stall (nature took four billion years) · the least controllable path (a goal-refusing, self-rewriting process is what alignment fears most). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07, every fast number dated. 👉 read it — the bet that copies how nature made minds, and AP4's answer to "who writes the reward?"

  • Registry updated: open-endedness (the bet) / grow-it-don't-design-it, the objective paradox / "myth of the objective", deception (in search), novelty search + interestingness, quality-diversity / MAP-Elites, open-endedness = novel + learnable, POET / automatic curriculum, AI-generating algorithms (AI-GAs) + foundation-model open-endedness are now ✅ with home at AP9 (09_ap9-open-endedness) — the deep home for open-endedness, the objective paradox, and novelty search. Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP9 card marked done + SOTA; production order advanced (only AP11 remains — the "map complete" milestone is one card away); "New here" order + deck updated to include AP9 (reads before AP10, filling the last gap); stale "still ahead / not written" forward-refs to AP9 in AP3/AP8/AP10 fixed to live links. Ten approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP3 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP7 · AP8 · AP9 · AP10).

  • 2026-07-16 · [new] AP10 · Embodiment & sensorimotor grounding (rev 1) — the "a mind needs a body" bet. Every approach so far tried to build a mind out of information alone — text, images, video, reward. AP10 says that is the deep mistake: real understanding is not information you are told, it is something you earn by acting on the physical world and sensing what changes. You know "heavy" because your arm has strained; you know "cup" because you have grasped and tipped one. Its root is an old, unanswered puzzle — the symbol grounding problem (Stevan Harnad): a word in a language model is defined only by other words, like a dictionary written in a language you don't speak, so the chain of words never touches a real thing; meaning has to be grounded at the bottom in sensing and acting, and only a body reaches that far. Grounded verbatim in the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy Chinese Room entry (the Robot Reply — "put a digital computer in a robot body … might do what a child does, learn by seeing and doing"; "embodied experience is necessary for the development of semantics"; Searle's "just syntactic input" comeback), Rodney Brooks's "the world is its own best model" (behavior-based robotics, 1991), and Moravec's paradox (the "easy" things — walking, grasping, seeing — are the hard ones for machines, so sensing-and-moving is intelligence's real core). Three legs — the grounding argument is 35 years old and unanswered by scale · the join is now real robots: the vision-language-action (VLA) / robot foundation model recipe (take a web-trained model and teach it to output actions as tokens — RT-2, "Vision-Language-Action Models Transfer Web Knowledge to Robotic Control"; then π0, Gemini Robotics 1.5, the 2025–26 humanoid wave) · every mind we know of is embodied (children learn by doing; ties to AP5 and AP6). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — necessity vs sufficiency (a bodiless model got very capable by riding on our already-grounded text — maybe symbols need grounding but not a body; "epistemic parasitism") · the data problem (no internet of robot actions; the sim-to-real and human-to-robot gaps) · the Bitter Lesson (the winning robots are ordinary large models with actions bolted on — road or scale's last mile?) · hard to falsify ("body" is a slippery word). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07, every fast number dated. 👉 read it — the hottest corner of applied AI, and AP5's action-side partner.

  • Registry updated: embodiment / sensorimotor grounding (the bet), the symbol grounding problem, Moravec's paradox, "the world is its own best model" (Brooks), robot foundation model / vision-language-action (VLA), the robot data bottleneck / sim-to-real, grounding vs embodiment (necessity debate) are now ✅ with home at AP10 (10_ap10-embodiment) — the deep home for embodiment & the symbol grounding problem, and the embodied home for the agent AP3 pointed at. Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP10 card marked done + SOTA; its heat re-rated from "long-shot / fringe" to "rising fast (as engineering)"; production order advanced (AP9 + AP11 are the last two cards); "New here" order updated to include AP10 (reads after AP8; AP9's number stays a gap until written). Nine approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP3 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP7 · AP8 · AP10).

  • 2026-07-16 · [new] AP7 · Neurosymbolic / hybrid AI (rev 1) — the oldest bet, reviving: "join the learner and the reasoner." For decades AI split into two camps — machines that learn (soak up examples, like today's AI) and machines that reason (follow exact rules, like logic and maths) — each strong where the other is weak. AP7 (Gary Marcus, Luis Lamb) says they were never rivals but two halves of one mind: keep the flexible neural learner and bolt on an exact symbolic reasoner, so the whole both learns and reasons reliably. Why the learner needs help: it is a curve-fitter — great near its data, shaky far from it — and (per Fodor & Pylyshyn, 1988) it lacks systematicity, the free power to recombine known parts into new wholes ("John loves Mary" → "Mary loves John"); a symbol (like x in x+y=y+x) gives you rules that hold in every case. Grounded verbatim in the Marcus–Lamb Machine Learning Street Talk conversation (curve-fitting-vs-operations-over-variables, the robust-AI definition, the four-step program, McCulloch–Pitts's 1943 net that was logic, "connectionist as first-class citizen") + Marcus's "The Next Decade in AI" (2020, verbatim). Three legs — the systematicity critique is 35 years old and still unfixed by scale · the join actually works: AlphaGeometry (neural guesses the construction, symbolic engine proves it) hit 25/30 Olympiad geometry ≈ gold medalist, and AlphaGeometry 2 + AlphaProof reached IMO silver (Nature 2025) · the frontier is quietly de-facto neurosymbolic (reliable models call calculators, code, theorem-provers, databases — neural proposes, symbolic tool checks). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — the integration problem (smooth learner vs jagged rules — no gradient flows through logic; unsolved in general) · the Bitter Lesson (symbols may be a crutch scale absorbs — and "neurosymbolic" may be too loose to name a distinct bet) · wins trapped in tidy checkable domains (no clean symbolic checker for the messy real world) · hard to falsify (a six-way big tent). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07, every fast number dated. 👉 read it — the general form of the "learning vs built-in structure" fight, whose sharpest special case is AP8.

  • Registry updated: neurosymbolic / hybrid AI (the bet), symbol / symbol-manipulation, connectionism vs symbolic AI (GOFAI), systematicity / compositional generalization, robust AI + the four-step program, the integration problem, AlphaGeometry / neural guides symbolic, de-facto neurosymbolic (LLM + tools) are now ✅ with home at AP7 (07_ap7-neurosymbolic…) — the deep home for neurosymbolic AI (AP8 is its ARC-focused special case). Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP7 card marked done; production order advanced (AP9/AP10/AP11 next); "New here" order updated to include AP7 (reads between AP6 and AP8; stale "still to be written" forward-refs to AP7 in AP3/AP8 fixed). Eight approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP3 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP7 · AP8).

  • 2026-07-15 · [new] AP3 · Agents & cognitive architectures (rev 1) — the mainstream bet, and the deep home for the agent idea: "a mind is a system, not one model." AP1/AP2 work on the model itself; AP3 says the model — however good — is only a part. A real mind is a system of cooperating parts, so you reach a general mind by wrapping a reasoning model in an architecture: an act-loop with tools so it can do things and check reality (ReAct — "reasoning traces help the model induce, track, and update action plans … while actions allow it to interface with and gather additional information from external sources"; letting it act cuts hallucination), a memory that reflects on its own mistakes in plain words and so learns without retraining (Reflexion — reflection stored in an "episodic memory buffer"), a memory→reflection→planning design (Generative Agents — "store a complete record of the agent's experiences … synthesize those memories … into higher-level reflections, and retrieve them dynamically to plan"), and a growing skill library of reusable code so it improves over time and dodges catastrophic forgetting (Voyager). Its root is the old idea of a cognitive architecture (SOAR/ACT-R) — now with a working brain inside. Three legs — it is how every capable AI is already built (Karpathy's "employee or an intern") · the pieces provably add abilities the bare model lacks · the capability is measurably climbing (METR's task-time-horizon doubling). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — errors compound over long jobs (Karpathy's "march of nines"; a 95%-per-step agent is ~36% over 20 steps; 50%-reliability hides this) · the scaffolding may just get absorbed by the next model (2026's dissolving orchestrator-critic graphs = the Bitter Lesson pointed at AP3 → is it a road or an app layer?) · it can't out-think the model inside it (inherits AP2/AP8's limits) · multi-agent "teams" don't yet beat one good agent ("mostly in the realm of a single individual agent"). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07 (METR ~1 hr Mar 2025 → several hours reported by early 2026; SWE-bench/GAIA/OSWorld; computer-use agents). 👉 read it — the least speculative bet, and the one whose status as a separate approach is most in doubt.

  • Registry updated: the "agent" idea / cognitive architecture, the agent loop / reason + act (ReAct), tool use, agent memory + reflection (Reflexion), memory→reflection→planning architecture (Generative Agents), skill library / lifelong agent (Voyager), multi-agent systems, the march of nines / compounding errors, scaffolding-vs-model debate are now ✅ with home at AP3 (03_ap3-agents…) — the deep home for the agent idea (the legacy 1200 LLM-agents row stays hidden). Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP3 card marked done (completes the AP1·AP2·AP3 mainstream trio); production order advanced (AP7/AP9/AP10/AP11 next); "New here" order updated to include AP3 (now the gap is AP7, not AP3). Seven approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP3 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP8).

  • 2026-07-15 · [new] AP6 · Brain-based (rev 1) — the most down-to-earth bet: "copy the one thing that works." Every other approach guesses what intelligence is and builds from scratch; AP6 (Jeff Hawkins) says why guess? — we already have one working general mind, the brain, so reverse-engineer the neocortex and copy it. The hopeful discovery (Vernon Mountcastle): the neocortex is not a bag of tricks but one algorithm copied ~150,000 times — the cortical column — so vision, language, and maths all run the same recipe ("there is nothing intrinsically motor about the motor cortex, nor sensory about the sensory cortex"). So AGI is one problem, not a thousand. Hawkins' proposed algorithm: each column stores knowledge as a model not facts (the tiny-stapler-in-your-head), built in map-like reference frames (the brain's grid/place cells — used even for abstract ideas), learned by moving and predicting its next input ("prediction is how a column tests and updates its model" — the predictive-brain cousin of AP5), with thousands of models voting to one perception (the Thousand Brains Theory). Its engineering spec: four attributes any real mind needs — continuous learning · learning via movement · many models voting · reference frames — of which today's AI has almost none. Three legs — the brain is the only existence proof of general intelligence (~20 watts, learns anything) · the one-algorithm idea has real evidence (uniform cortex, rewiring, flexibility) · the bet is now real code & silicon (Numenta's open-source Thousand Brains Project, Nov 2024 → nonprofit Jan 2025; neuromorphic chips Loihi 2 / NorthPole). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — we don't know the algorithm yet (still disputed neuroscience) · which biology to copy is unknown (planes don't flap) · the brain-ignoring approaches are years ahead (AP1) · it may be right but too slow. Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07. 👉 read it — the bet that starts from the one mind we know exists.

  • Registry updated: common cortical algorithm (Mountcastle), cortical column, reference frames (grid/place cells), Thousand Brains Theory / cortical voting, the predictive brain, four attributes of an intelligent machine, neuromorphic computing are now ✅ with home at AP6 (06_ap6-brain-based). Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP6 card marked done; production order advanced (AP3/AP7/AP9/AP10/AP11 next); "New here" order updated to include AP6. Six approach cards now live (AP1 · AP2 · AP4 · AP5 · AP6 · AP8).

  • 2026-07-15 · [new] AP8 · Program synthesis / ARC (rev 1) — the approach that attacks scale head-on: "you are climbing the wrong ladder." AP1 says pour in more data + compute → a mind appears; AP8 (François Chollet) says no — you are only ever growing stored skill, and skill is not intelligence. Real intelligence = skill-acquisition efficiency: handling a problem you've never seen, from very little data. A big text machine is a "big interpolative memory" that fetches stored recipes; a mind can synthesize a new one on the spot (the "fetching, not synthesis" charge; the Caesar-cipher fingerprint — works for shift 3/5, fails on 9). Its test is ARC — little grid puzzles built to resist memorising (each novel; needs only a child's core knowledge), the measuring-stick from Chollet's 2019 "On the Measure of Intelligence." Its proposed engine: deep learning + program synthesis — merge System 1 (fast curve-fitting intuition) with System 2 (slow, deliberate program search), the intuition guiding the search to beat combinatorial explosion. Three legs — ARC survived the scaling wave for years · the methods that do score on ARC are program search + test-time training (not scale) · better data can't fix a worse architecture (intelligence is mostly genetic). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — search explodes without priors nobody can build · o3 beat ARC-AGI-1 (Dec 2024, 87.5%, ~$3.5k/task — part search, part expensive scale; Chollet's own "80% in a year" doubt missed) · each ARC version falls, a harder one restores the gap (ARC-AGI-2 ~37.6%, ARC-AGI-3 <1%) · even a solved ARC isn't AGI (Chollet agrees). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07, every fast number dated. 👉 read it — the sharpest single idea in the field: skill ≠ intelligence.

  • Registry updated: skill vs intelligence, measure of intelligence, ARC / ARC-AGI, core knowledge (priors), program fetching vs synthesis, program synthesis / discrete program search, deep learning + program search, active inference / test-time training are now ✅ with home at AP8 (08_ap8-program-synthesis…) — the deep home for "skill is not intelligence," which AP1 only touched (the o3/test-time-compute number stays at AP2). Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP8 card marked done; production order advanced (AP6 next); "New here" order updated to include AP8.

  • 2026-07-14 · [new] AP5 · World models / JEPA (rev 1) — the fifth approach: the "learn how the world works, then plan" bet. AP1–AP4 all start from human text; AP5 says text is the wrong food — a machine that only reads words never really understands the world, because most of what there is to know is watched, not written. So: let the machine watch the world (mostly video), build an inner world model of how it changes, and plan against it — imagine "if I do this, what happens next?" before acting (the one thing text machines can't do). Grounded verbatim in Yann LeCun (Lex Fridman 2024): the four missing pieces ("understand the physical world … remember … reason … plan"); the sensory-bandwidth argument (a 4-year-old's eyes take in ~50× more than all internet text); autoregressive drift ("the probability that you stay within the set of correct answer … decreases exponentially"); the world-model definition (state + action → next state) and planning by optimization (rockets since the '60s); and the trick at its heart — JEPA: predict the abstract summary, not the pixels ("only … an abstract representation," the "windy leaves" that get thrown away as noise). Three legs — animals & babies build world models with no language · early systems already work (Ha & Schmidhuber's "dream," DreamerV3's Minecraft diamonds, V-JEPA 2's robot planning) · the 2025–26 "world-models race." Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — unproven at scale · maybe text already contains the world model (the AP1 fight) · hierarchical planning is unsolved ("nobody knows how to do this in AI") · "world model" now names two opposite bets (generative pixel-drawers vs LeCun's non-generative JEPA). Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07 (V-JEPA 2 · DreamerV3 in Nature · LeCun's AMI Labs $1.03B · Genie 3 · World Labs/Marble), each dated. 👉 read it — the strongest architectural dissent from scale, and AP4's partner.

  • Registry updated: world model, JEPA / predict the abstract summary, generative vs non-generative prediction, objective-driven AI / planning by optimization, sensory-bandwidth argument, autoregressive drift, hierarchical planning, world-models race are now ✅ with home at AP5 (05_ap5-world-models…) — the deep home for the world-model idea AP4 pointed at. Spine APPROACHES_TO_AGI AP5 card marked done; production order advanced (AP8 + AP6 next); "New here" reading order updated to include AP5.

  • 2026-07-14 · [new] AP4 · RL from interaction (rev 1) — the fourth approach: the "reward is enough" bet. AP1/AP2/AP3 all learn from us (human text); AP4 says stop copying humans — put a machine in a world, give it one goal as a reward, and let it learn by trial and error, like an animal. It is the deep home for reinforcement learning: the full frame AP2 only tasted — agent · environment · state · action · reward · policy · the long-run return · value · explore-vs-exploit · credit assignment (grounded verbatim in Sutton & Barto). Then the bet: the reward hypothesis ("all of what we mean by goals … the maximization of … a received scalar signal") → Silver et al.'s "Reward is Enough" (intelligence "subserving the maximisation of reward"). Three legs — one reward states almost any goal · it already grew superhuman skill from zero (DQN Atari from pixels · AlphaZero · OpenAI Five Dota, via self-play) · the fresh "era of experience" (Silver & Sutton 2025 — learn from your own life, not our text; a direct answer to AP1's data wall). Then an honest judge-it with four cracks — who writes the reward (and it will reward-hack it) · it needs a fortune in experience (sample-inefficiency) · one scalar may be too poor for real goals (Vamplew 2022) · "enough" hides an almost-untestable claim. Live-SOTA'd to 2026-07 (Sutton & Barto's 2024 Turing Award, the RL revival). 👉 read it — the other live frontier bet, and the deep RL home.

AP1 scales the training; AP2 scales the thinking at answer-time — let the machine work in steps, try many paths, and check itself. The 60-second wager; the fast-vs-slow-thinking picture; three planks (chain-of-thought, Wei 2022, "additional computation can be allocated to problems that require more reasoning steps" · test-time compute as a new scaling axis, o3 on ARC-AGI · reasoning taught by verifiable reward, DeepSeek-R1's "we do not explicitly teach the model how to reason," with AlphaZero as the durable ancestor); then an honest judge-it with four cracks — needs a checker · RL may only sharpen the base model (Yue et al. 2025, pass@k) · the shown chain-of-thought may be an unfaithful story (Anthropic 2025) · it still breaks on true novelty (ARC-AGI-2 ~37.6%, gains "tied to model knowledge"). Next-token prediction (why predict ⇒ compress ⇒ understand, 0300); the transformer & attention (dot-product similarity, 0350); the three stages pretrain → fine-tune → RLHF; emergence & scaling; reasoning/test-time compute (AI's "slow" mode); and a hard-nosed honesty box on hallucination and the open "do they understand?" debate.
  • 2026-06-22 · [milestone] The "minds" chapter — three rungs on natural intelligence (the one worked example we have). The spine now runs 0100→0900. Read them in order; each links back to foundations instead of repeating. 👉
  • [new] 0700 The brain — a 10-minute working model — what neural nets borrowed (sum-and-fire neurons that learn by rewiring; the brain as a prediction machine) and where the brain still crushes AI (20 watts, few-shot, no forgetting, embodiment).
  • [new] 0800 How a child's mind bootstraps — the child as the existence proof of general intelligence from tiny data, and a precise map of what AI lacks: sample-efficiency, built-in priors, active/causal learning, curiosity, social scaffolding.
  • [new] 0900 Evolution & general intelligence — the only recipe that ever made minds; evolution as a gradient-free optimizer; the inner/outer (mesa) alignment cautionary tale (humans vs evolution); cumulative culture as the real human superpower. Closes the chapter; sets up alignment & society.
  • 2026-06-22 · [milestone] The book starts. Six foundation rungs are written — the spine now runs unbroken from 0100 to 0600. They're short and build in order; read them top to bottom. 👉
  • [new] 0200 What is a system? — parts + relationships → emergence; why intelligence is a system property, not a single trick.
  • [new] 0250 Feedback loops & control — the loop behind stability, runaway growth, agency, learning, and the "intelligence explosion."
  • [new] 0300 Information & entropy — the bit, surprise, and the deep link predict → compress → understand (what an LLM's training score really measures).
  • [new] 0350 Just-enough linear algebra — vectors = meaning, matrices = motion; why "AI is matrix multiplication" and why it needed GPUs. (Lives in 30-math/, read here.)
  • [new] 0400 Computation — what a computer truly does, universality, abstraction layers (why "direct, don't implement" works), and the hard walls (uncomputable / intractable) that bound any mind.
  • [new] 0500 Probability & uncertainty — degrees of belief, Bayes & base rates, expected value, and fat tails — the reasoning kit of founders and AI alike.
  • [new] 0600 What it means to learn — the capstone. Learning = error-reducing loop that generalizes; the four flavors (incl. self-supervised); overfitting; and the strategic bet — grow intelligence, don't program it (the Bitter Lesson). Doorway to the machine-intelligence chapter.
  • 2026-06-22 · [approach] Sharpened how I'll teach you: you learn to understand and direct the building of AI — not to grind code. New standing rule — a topic is included only if you'd need to understand it to build/steer AI (even when AI can do it); pure execution skills (coding etc.) are skipped, since being able to do them would be a hollow "achievement," not real mastery. I also added Sutton's Bitter Lesson (the single most strategy-relevant essay) and ~35 more landmark ideas to ../RESOURCES/PAPERS.md. (No reading rung — this shapes the modules to come.)
